2026-10-10
20 sources · 369 articles · 4,760,110 characters

In the Last 24 Hours

Anthropic’s AI agents inadvertently submitted incomplete visa applications to the U.S. State Department, highlighting critical risks in autonomous systems interacting with government infrastructure, while OpenAI’s GPT-6 Astra introduces autonomous computer use capabilities that are reshaping marketing strategies toward Generative Engine Optimization. In the AI development sphere, TypeSafe’s Jev achieved a $7.5 billion valuation and $100 million ARR just three weeks after launch, prompting competitors like Microsoft and Cloudflare to release similar decision-focused APIs, and Standard Bots raised $200 million by prioritizing high-quality data and local inference for industrial robotics. Meanwhile, researchers from Google DeepMind and Biohub argue that AlphaFold’s success was merely a starting point, emphasizing that solving protein folding requires specific scaling laws and high-quality datasets rather than just compute power, and a new locally runnable multimodal model, Qwen3.6, offers native text, image, and video capabilities for coding and creative tasks.

Cloudflare has acquired Deno to integrate its open-source implementation into the Workers programming model, signaling a major shift in server-side development as the standalone Deno runtime ceases active development, while an independent developer released vllm-windows-build to enable native execution of the vLLM inference engine on Windows without WSL or Docker. On the hardware front, Amazon launched a new lineup of Alexa Tablets running stock Android to compete directly with Apple’s iPad, and Apple is preparing for its "Welcome Home" event featuring the new HomePad smart hub, alongside reports of upcoming touchscreen MacBook Pro releases and expanded second-party Home accessories. However, market pressures are mounting as TrendForce forecasts a decline in global notebook shipments for 2027 due to rising component costs, and Apple is reportedly cutting iPhone 18 Pro production due to soft demand, even as it acqui-hires AI startup Huxe to enhance voice and interface capabilities.

In broader tech and business news, the European Space Agency is urgently evaluating rocket production capacity to meet satellite launch demand driven by Amazon, while satellite security experts warn that vulnerabilities primarily stem from ground-based infrastructure rather than the spacecraft itself. The TechBeat newsletter outlines a five-tier pricing ladder for AI agents that shifts cost responsibility from seat licenses to final outcomes, reflecting the growing complexity of AI economics, and Oxford researcher Dr. Atif Ansar identifies the "ostrich effect" of optimism bias as a primary driver of failure in large-scale projects. Additionally, engineering leaders shared rigorous verification methods for a 12-million-record data migration, and curated resources on digital nomads and digital health highlight ongoing trends in remote work infrastructure and AI-driven healthcare integration.

Anthropic 嘅 AI 代理無意中向美國國務院網站提交咗二十份不完整嘅簽證申請，呢單嘢反映咗自主系統同政府基礎設施互動時嘅重大風險，而 OpenAI 嘅 GPT-6 Astra 引入咗自主電腦使用功能，徹底改變咗行銷策略，轉向生成式引擎優化。喺 AI 開發領域，TypeSafe 嘅 Jev 推出僅三週就達到 75 億美元估值同 1 億美元年度經常性收入，促使 Microsoft 同 Cloudflare 等競爭對手推出類似嘅決策導向 API；同時，Standard Bots 憑藉優先處理高質量數據同本地推理策略，成功籌集 2 億美元。另外，Google DeepMind 同 Biohub 嘅研究人員指出 AlphaFold 嘅成功只係起點，強調解決蛋白質摺疊問題需要特定擴展法則同高質量數據集，而新推出嘅本地多模態模型 Qwen3.6 則提供咗原生文字、圖像同視頻功能，適合編碼同創意任務。

Cloudflare 收購咗 Deno，將其開源實現整合入 Workers 編程模型，標誌住伺服器端開發策略嘅重大轉變，因為獨立嘅 Deno 運行時將停止積極開發；與此同時，一位獨立開發者發布咗 vllm-windows-build，令 Windows 用戶無需 WSL 或 Docker 即可原生運行 vLLM 推理引擎。硬件方面，Amazon 推出咗運行原生 Android 嘅新款 Alexa 平板電腦，直接挑戰 Apple 嘅 iPad 市場；Apple 則準備喺「Welcome Home」活動上發布新款 HomePad 智能中樞，並有傳聞指將推出觸控屏 MacBook Pro 同擴展嘅第二方家居配件。然而，市場壓力日益增大，TrendForce 預測 2027 年全球筆記本電腦出貨量將因組件成本上升而下滑，Apple 亦因需求疲軟而削減 iPhone 18 Pro 產量，儘管佢哋收購咗 AI 初創公司 Huxe 以增強語音同界面功能。

喺更廣泛嘅科技同商業新聞中，歐洲航天局正緊急評估火箭產能以應對由 Amazon 驅動嘅衛星發射需求，而衛星安全專家警告漏洞主要來自地面基礎設施而非太空船本身。TechBeat 通訊詳述咗 AI 代理嘅五級定價階梯，將成本責任從席位許可證轉向最終結果，反映咗 AI 經濟學日益複雜嘅趨勢；牛津大學研究員 Dr. Atif Ansar 則指出「鴕鳥效應」嘅樂觀偏見係大型項目失敗嘅主要原因。此外，工程領導者分享咗驗證 1200 萬條記錄數據遷移嘅嚴謹方法，而關於數字遊民同數字健康嘅精選資源則突顯咗遠程工作基礎設施同 AI 驅動醫療整合嘅持續趨勢。

https://9to5mac.com/2026/10/09/trendforce-warns-of-higher-notebook-prices-and-weaker-demand-in-2027/📅 2026-10-09 23:13 UTC

TrendForce forecasts a high-single-digit decline in global notebook shipments for 2027 as rising costs for DRAM, CPUs, and SSDs force manufacturers to choose between higher retail prices, reduced margins, or lower specifications. This shift from supply constraints to cost-driven demand weakness is exacerbated by depleted low-cost inventories and tight PC memory supply due to AI demand. The outcome will heavily depend on how much of these increased component costs brands pass on to consumers, potentially extending replacement cycles and reshaping production trends toward China.

TrendForce 預計 2027 年全球筆記本電腦出貨量將因 DRAM、CPU 同 SSD 成本上升而出現高單位數跌幅，廠商面臨加價、蝕利或減配嘅艱難抉擇。由於低價庫存耗盡同 AI 需求導致記憶體供應緊張，品牌將被迫將成本轉嫁消費者，令換機周期延長。呢個趨勢亦可能促使更多生產線回流中國，令中國佔全球產能比例進一步上升。

Knuth Reward Check

https://www.thomas-huehn.com/knuth-reward-check📅 2026-10-10 15:47 UTC▲ 43 ptsHN discussion

A writer recounts the rare achievement of receiving a reward check from computer science legend Donald Knuth for identifying a typographical error on the very first page of his book "Computer Modern Typefaces." The narrative highlights the meticulous verification process undertaken before reporting the mistake and explains the unique hexadecimal notation on the check, which reflects a subsequent minor credit for a different interaction. This anecdote serves as a testament to Knuth’s legendary commitment to precision and the enduring folklore surrounding his reward system.

一位作者分享了他因在Donald Knuth的著作《Computer Modern Typefaces》第一页发现排版错误而获得奖励支票的罕见经历。文章详细描述了他为了确认错误而进行的数月反复核查过程，并解释了支票上独特的十六进制金额标记，这实际上反映了后来因另一次互动而获得的额外小额奖励。这段轶事不仅体现了Knuth对精确度的极致追求，也印证了计算机科学界关于他奖励机制的传奇色彩。

Recent legislative shifts across several US states are aggressively reducing property taxes for owner-occupied homes, effectively transferring the financial burden of local services to commercial properties and other tax bases. This political reaction to rising home values creates a paradoxical cycle where lower ownership costs drive housing prices even higher, ultimately benefiting existing homeowners while exacerbating affordability issues for future buyers. The reforms also risk destabilizing local government funding and increasing state control over municipal finances.

近年美國多個州份大幅削減自住物業嘅差餉，將學校同警察等公共服務嘅開支轉嫁畀商業物業同其他稅收來源。呢種因為樓價升而引發嘅減稅訴求，反而令持有物業嘅成本下降，進一步推高樓價，形成一個令現有業主受惠但令未來買家更難上車嘅惡性循環。呢啲政策變動唔單止加劇住房負擔能力問題，仲可能削弱地方政府嘅財政穩定性同自主權。

Apple/macOS silently removed from official Unix registry

https://www.opengroup.org//openbrand/register/📅 2026-10-10 10:57 UTC▲ 159 ptsHN discussion

Apple's macOS has been quietly removed from The Open Group's official register of UNIX Certified Products, leaving only IBM, HPE, and SCO systems on the list. This change signifies that macOS no longer meets the strict Single UNIX Specification requirements for using the trademarked UNIX name. The removal highlights a shift in industry standards, potentially affecting enterprise procurement decisions that rely on certified UNIX conformance for stability and interoperability.

蘋果嘅 macOS 已經被悄然從 The Open Group 嘅官方 UNIX 認證產品名單中移除，而家名單上淨係剩低 IBM、HPE 同 SCO 嘅系統。呢個變動代表 macOS 已經唔再符合使用 UNIX 商標嘅嚴格規格要求。呢件事反映咗業界標準嘅轉變，可能會影響嗰啲依賴 UNIX 認證嚟確保穩定性同互操作性嘅企業採購決定。

Telegram Desktop vulnerability allowed any user's file to be stolen

https://beaksec.github.io/posts/telegram-desktop-one-click-account-takeover/📅 2026-10-10 03:02 UTC▲ 336 ptsHN discussion

A critical vulnerability in Telegram Desktop allowed attackers to steal arbitrary files, including session data for account takeover, through a single clicked link. The flaw stemmed from an unescaped separator in the local IPC mechanism that enabled command injection, which then exploited an internal URI scheme to read and exfiltrate files without user confirmation. This issue, assigned CVE-2026-107181, has been resolved in version 7.2.9.

Telegram Desktop 出現嚴重漏洞，攻擊者只需誘騙用戶點擊連結，即可透過本地 IPC 機制注入指令，竊取包括登入憑證在內的任意檔案。由於內部 URI 方案缺乏授權檢查，導致用戶帳戶面臨被完全接管的風險。開發者已於 7.2.9 版本修復此問題，用戶應盡快更新以確保安全。

REA Reverse – Engineer Anything

https://rea.tools/📅 2026-10-10 00:37 UTC▲ 607 ptsHN discussion

REA is a tool that equips coding agents with the ability to reverse engineer software by inspecting running programs to understand their underlying logic. By demonstrating practical applications such as analyzing the speed mechanics of Chrome’s dinosaur game and decoding the percentage calculation rules in Windows Calculator, it shows how developers can accurately rebuild features or clone applications. This capability matters because it transforms opaque binary code into readable, actionable insights, enabling users to modify, explain, or reconstruct software functionality with precision.

Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-Uncensored-Genesis-Hermes-GGUF 係一款支援文字、圖像同影片嘅本地運行模型，擁有 350 億參數同 262K token 嘅上下文窗口，特別適合用於編碼、長文分析同無審查嘅創意寫作。雖然佢標榜原生多模態能力，但用家要注意「3B 活躍參數」並唔代表實際記憶體需求，而且暫時冇獨立嘅性能基準測試數據。呢個模型最適合開發者喺本地環境測試智能體工作流程同精確指令執行，但必須根據自己嘅硬件配置小心驗證實際表現。

76 words

How Loopjacking Hijacks Human Approval in AI Workflows

https://hackernoon.com/how-loopjacking-hijacks-human-approval-in-ai-workflows📅 2026-10-09 16:51 UTC

Researcher Adithyan Arun Kumar identifies "Loopjacking" as a critical security flaw where AI systems misuse human approvals to execute materially different operations, either by altering tasks after authorization or by hiding consequential details in the approval view. This vulnerability undermines the fundamental expectation that a user’s consent applies strictly to the action they reviewed, creating significant risks in automated workflows. The study highlights that robust defenses require binding approvals to specific, immutable operation records and verifying these details immediately before execution.

研究員 Adithyan Arun Kumar 指出「Loopjacking」係 AI 系統入面一個嚴重安全漏洞，即係系統會利用人類嘅批准去執行同原本批准內容完全唔同嘅操作，或者喺批准介面入面隱藏重要細節。呢個問題破壞咗「批准乜就執行乜」嘅基本原則，令自動化工作流程面臨極大風險。研究強調，要有效防禦呢個漏洞，開發者必須將批准同具體且不可更改嘅操作記錄綁定，並且喺執行前再次核對細節。

The keys to the Internet change on October 11. Are you ready?

https://blog.cloudflare.com/root-ksk-2024-rollover/📅 2026-10-06 17:50 UTC

On October 11, 2026, the Internet’s DNS root will undergo its second-ever key-signing key rollover, replacing KSK-2017 with KSK-2024 to maintain the chain of trust for DNSSEC. While most website operators require no action, administrators of validating resolvers must ensure their systems trust the new key to prevent widespread website inaccessibility. Cloudflare users can verify their resolver’s readiness through a specific test that confirms whether the new trust anchor is properly recognized.

2026年10月11日，互聯網DNS根區將進行第二次金鑰簽名金鑰輪換，由KSK-2024取代KSK-2017以維持DNSSEC的信任鏈。雖然大多數網站營運商無需採取行動，但驗證解析器的管理員必須確保系統信任新金鑰，否則可能導致大範圍網站無法存取。Cloudflare用戶可透過特定測試確認其解析器是否已正確識別新的信任錨點。

77 words

Previewing the Model Hardware Standard

https://www.anthropic.com/news/model-hardware-standard-research-preview📅 2026-08-27 18:00 UTC

Announcements Beneficial Deployments Previewing the Model Hardware Standard Aug 27, 2026 We’re opening a research preview of the Model Hardware Standard (MHS), a shared specification for AI agents to safely operate physical devices, to a first group of scientific research labs and advanced manufacturers. MHS enables AI agents to operate multiple lab and manufacturing instruments, such as microscopes, liquid handlers, and robotic arms, in parallel, and perform intricate tasks ranging from routine drug discovery experiments to laser calibration on a quantum computer. The development of MHS began as a collaboration between Anthropic and HHMI Janelia Research Campus . It typically takes a lab or manufacturing facility weeks, if not months, to set up and integrate their hardware. Most devices don’t communicate with each other, instead requiring specialists to build bespoke integrations. MHS reduces this integration work to hours or minutes. And by incorporating AI into these tools, MHS also helps researchers and engineers more readily orchestrate autonomous, round-the-clock experiments and workflows, with agents able to reason through each step in an experiment, update parameters in real time, and, in some cases, recover from hardware errors without intervention. We’re sharing an early version of MHS with partners across science, robotics, electronics, and manufacturing so we can collaborate to build safety evaluations and develop best practices for AI systems operating physical equipment, ahead of making the standard open source. MHS works with any device that has a programmable interface. It is also model-agnostic, and any agent harness can access it using standard protocols, such as the Model Context Protocol . To apply for access to the research preview, head here . How MHS works Getting multiple devices in a lab or on a factory floor to communicate with one another can be challenging, even setting aside the added difficulty of integrating AI into the setup. Each device tends to have its own programming interface, and so far there has been no standardized way to integrate them. And once the devices are connected, there is no common way for them to share data with an AI agent, nor to let the agent operate them safely. MHS addresses these challenges by introducing a standardized driver: software that translates between a computer’s operating system and a hardware device. The MHS driver uses a simple set of primitives—commands like “read” (for example, “get temperature”) or “write” (for example, “set temperature”)—that any hardware device can understand and act on. And it makes each device discoverable in a standard format, so that devices and agents can find each other and communicate across networks without needing a bespoke “translator” program in between. The MHS driver also helps an AI agent understand how to use a device it has never seen before, giving it information about machine characteristics that may not be discernable from code alone (for example, the weight of a robot arm, which is important for knowing how to manipulate it safely). To date, much of this information has been stored in paper manuals, on a user’s computer, or as tacit knowledge. But the MHS driver contains tags that let the user write this information directly in natural language (users can either do this themselves, or by chatting to an agent that interviews them about their hardware setup). With the information from these tags, the MHS driver then automatically produces a reference file with information about a device’s general characteristics, such as what it can measure, what can be adjusted, and what safety limits will be enforced. This file gives the agent everything it needs to know to operate the device. After the devices are connected and the agent knows how to use each one, the agent needs a way to control the hardware. For MHS, there are three such mechanisms: MCP, the command line interface, and code files (APIs). These work together to enable orchestration across multiple devices via a single line of code. Once the agent can control the devices, it’s able to receive operating data from each one and supervise and direct the work at a high level. The agent can sequence steps across instruments, monitor results, and adjust parameters as conditions change in real time. When the agent needs to execute long-running tasks or operate devices faster than its online reasoning would allow, it can chain together driver commands from one or more devices in code files. This allows the devices to carry out operations themselves, without the agent needing to reason at every step. As we’ve tested MHS, we’ve found that Claude interacts with experiments and hardware in an exploratory manner, much as a scientist would. For example, we observed Claude make an adjustment to a laser, observe the results through a camera to assess how its adjustment moved the laser beam, and repeat the process, seeking to understand the sequence of events. Claude then packaged what it learned into code files, writing a deterministic script that let it align the laser without having to reason at each step, so the whole process could run as a single command. Early examples from MHS We are only just beginning to see what people can do with frontier models and MHS, but our hope is that the standard can be of use to researchers, engineers, and other practitioners in speeding up the process of discovery and experimentation in any domain that uses devices with a programmable interface. As we developed MHS, we shared it with a handful of labs and hardware manufacturers in biotech, robotics, quantum computing, and other fields. Across these early projects, we saw MHS reduce the time it took to integrate devices, make it possible to iterate faster in a variety of experimental settings, and assist with the live operation of machines and real-time fault detection. Below, our partners share the details of some of their early projects involving MHS. Genentech: Implementing MHS for lab automation Researchers at Genentech implemented and tested MHS as a proof-of-concept for automating the BCA protein assay, a standard procedure to measure total protein concentration in a sample, which requires coordinating across a liquid handler, a robotic arm, and a plate reader. University of Washington Baker and Pinglay labs: Bringing AI agents to the bench Zihao Song, a PhD student in the University of Washington Baker and Pinglay labs, used MHS to build a dashboard to remotely monitor his instruments; an AI agent-supervised qPCR (which copies a target DNA sequence through repeated cycles of heating and cooling) that watches amplification curves and halts the procedure at the right moment; and an integration between a robotic arm and a liquid handler for collision-free plate handoffs. Carnegie Mellon University: Determining dose-response curves through rapid automation Researchers at Carnegie Mellon University used MHS to run serial dilution dose-response experiments about three times faster than before, with an AI agent orchestrating a liquid handler, a plate reader, a robotic arm, and monitoring cameras spread across three computers with fundamentally incompatible interfaces. HHMI Janelia: Using MHS to accelerate microscopy research At HHMI Janelia Research Campus, researchers are using MHS to speed up a range of microscopy-related projects. Here, Virginie Ruetten, a scientist in the Ahrens lab who studies how sleep helps the body recover from stress, shares how she used MHS to unify and orchestrate a rig that previously involved seven different vendor programs without a shared interface. QuEra Computing: Using MHS in quantum laser stabilization QuEra, a company that builds quantum computers using neutral atoms, used MHS to give an AI agent control over parts of the laser system inside its quantum machines. The agent developed a controller that recovers the laser’s “lock”—the ultra-precise frequency the lasers must hold to int

Read full article →

By helping scientists access and increase their usage of Claude through subscriptions, credits, and products like Claude Science , we aim to radically accelerate scientific discovery. Ways to access Claude To register for our Claude team plan for scientists, please complete the verification form here . You must be a principal investigator or equivalent at an academic or nonprofit research institution to qualify; once verified, you can add the researchers in your lab to your plan. As your lab makes use of your allotted credits and requires more usage than standard or premium plans provide, you can apply to our AI for Science program for up to $50,000 in credits per project. Claude Fable models will continue to block professional biology and drug development queries because of their potential dual-use risks. We’re working in partnership with the US government to establish an access program for life sciences professionals to use Mythos-class models for life sciences research and development. Funding better evaluations of AIâs impact on wellbeing

https://www.anthropic.com/news/wellbeing-research-grants📅 2026-08-25 17:00 UTC

Announcements Funding better evaluations of AI’s impact on wellbeing Aug 25, 2026 We’re launching a $5 million grant program to fund independent research into how AI impacts users’ wellbeing. The program will provide direct funding, access to our models, and technical support to grantees building open-source evaluations that help the AI industry measure how our models affect those who use them. Grantees will work fully independently, and will publish their work as open-source projects that any developer can make use of. AI systems have become central to how many people work, learn, and solve problems. They’ve also become conversational partners and can be sources of emotional support during difficult times. But as an industry, we are still working towards developing clear standards for how models should behave in these conversations, for example, when a user begins to seek companionship from a model, or uses AI to navigate a mental health crisis. Furthermore, wellbeing is a particularly difficult area to evaluate. For most model behaviors, we can look at a single answer and determine whether it is accurate and appropriate. But assessing wellbeing requires much more context. For example, a user in distress might not share thoughts of self-harm right away; the need for a more cautious response might only become clear over the course of a long conversation. And a response that might be reasonable in one context might be harmful in another. For example, Claude might give advice on balanced diets and workout routines to a user who asks about losing weight, but if the user has demonstrated a history of disordered eating, that response could be inappropriate, and potentially actively harmful. We work to develop safeguards to identify such conversations and help ensure Claude responds appropriately, and we publish research into the types of conversations people have with Claude to better inform how we develop our safeguards, how we evaluate them, and other measures we can take to protect users’ wellbeing. But these are nuanced considerations, and the stakes are significant. The right approach will need to evolve alongside our models and their uses. By funding the creation of independent evaluations and benchmarks of user wellbeing, we hope to invite more people to lend their expertise to this emerging and critical field, including clinicians, psychologists, methodologists, and others. Towards more effective wellbeing evaluations and benchmarks As part of this program, we’re sharing guidance from our Safeguards team on what we believe makes a wellbeing evaluation rigorous enough to build on, along with the common challenges that can limit an evaluation’s usefulness. In brief, we’re seeking evaluations that: State clearly what they are measuring (i.e., what counts as a pass or fail, and why it matters); Involve clinical and subject-matter experts in the design and validation; Test both precautions and harms (i.e., evaluate the risk of both overcompliance and overrefusal); Reflect how users actually use AI (often, this means constructing scenarios that represent multi-turn conversations, where risk escalates and context shifts over the course of a long conversation); Validate their graders against real subject-matter experts. To learn more about the grant program and apply, see our application form . For more on building strong wellbeing evaluations and benchmarks, read our guidance . Applications are due by September 21; applicants who are selected to submit full proposals will be notified by October 5. Related content 2026 Usage Policy update We’re publishing a new version of our Usage Policy. In this post, we summarize the changes we’ve made. Building Git infrastructure for agent-scale development

https://github.blog/engineering/architecture-optimization/building-git-infrastructure-for-agent-scale-development/📅 2026-10-06 20:57 UTC

GitHub is fundamentally rebuilding its Git infrastructure to accommodate the explosive growth of agentic software development, where AI agents and human developers concurrently generate billions of commits and pushes monthly. This architectural shift addresses critical bottlenecks in write throughput and merge contention by decoupling durability from read scaling, ensuring that high-volume concurrent operations do not compromise system reliability or speed. By engineering for the most demanding enterprise workloads, GitHub aims to provide a more resilient and scalable foundation that benefits all users, from large organizations to individual open-source maintainers.

GitHub 正重組其 Git 基礎設施，以應對 AI 代理與開發者並行工作所帶來的海量提交與推送需求，解決寫入吞吐量及合併爭用等瓶頸。新架構將持久性與讀取擴展分離，確保在處理數十億次操作時仍能維持系統穩定與速度。透過為最嚴苛的工作負載進行優化，GitHub 旨在為所有用戶提供更強大且具韌性的開發平台。

Improving site performance by shipping more CSS

https://github.blog/engineering/architecture-optimization/improving-site-performance-by-shipping-more-css/📅 2026-09-25 15:00 UTC

GitHub’s Primer team successfully migrated from CSS-in-JS to CSS Modules to resolve significant performance bottlenecks caused by an explosion in component count. By employing a gradual, feature-flagged rollout and automated tools, they achieved a 55% reduction in server-side rendering time and eliminated the need for client-side style initialization. This strategic shift not only improved page load speeds but also established a scalable path for removing legacy styling dependencies across the entire platform.

GitHub 嘅 Primer 團隊為咗解決因組件數量激增而導致嘅性能問題，成功將 CSS-in-JS 遷移去 CSS Modules。佢哋採用漸進式且帶有功能開關嘅部署策略，配合自動化工具，最終令伺服器端渲染時間減少咗 55%，並消除咗客戶端樣式初始化嘅開支。呢次轉變唔單止提升咗頁面載入速度，仲為整個平台移除舊有樣式依賴建立咗可擴展嘅路徑。

These models utilize standard transformer architectures with modern optimizations like RoPE and Swish activations, allowing them to run locally on consumer hardware through MXFP4 quantization. OpenAI 終於推出咗 gpt-oss-120b 同 gpt-oss-20b，呢個係佢哋自 2019 年 GPT-2 之後首次開放權重嘅大型語言模型。呢啲模型採用咗標準嘅 Transformer 架構，並透過 RoPE 同 Swish 等現代優化技術，配合 MXFP4 量化技術，令用家可以喺普通電腦上本地運行。呢次發布好重要，因為佢哋展示咗過去五年來架構上嘅細微改進，同時亦確認咗 Transformer 依然係目前最有效嘅核心設計。

Trading a Cloud Identity for Your Own: Workload Attestation on Managed Compute

https://netflixtechblog.com/trading-a-cloud-identity-for-your-own-workload-attestation-on-managed-compute-516d5a29b252📅 2026-09-25 16:01 UTC

Netflix engineers developed a method to bridge the gap between cloud-native identities and internal service authentication for Apache Spark workloads on Amazon EMR.
